Legal basis: 
Section 96(1) and section 97(2) of the Act on Electronic Communications Services  
(917/2014). 
Provisions on sanctions for operations violating this Regulation are laid down in:  
Section 348(1) of the Act on Electronic Communications Services (917/2014). 
Implemented EU legislation:  
The European Commission will be notified of the Regulation in accordance with Directive 
(EU) 2015/1535. 
Modification details:  
The amendments made to the previous Regulation are listed as part of the Frequency 
Allocation Table appended to the Regulation. 
 
This Regulation repeals the previous Regulation 4 AF/2025M issued on 27 March 2025. 
RADIO FREQUENCY REGULATION 4 AG/2026M 
1 Scope of application 
This Regulation applies to the radio frequency spectrum 100 Hz – 400 GHz. 
Radio transmitters intended for use on the radio frequencies must meet the 
requirements of this Regulation for transmitting and receiving frequencies, 
channel spacing, bandwidth of transmission, duplex separation, transmitted 
powers and other corresponding radio characteristics (radio interfaces). 
Electrical equipment other than radio equipment (ISM equipment), 
designated to generate radio frequency energy and used for scientific, 
industrial, medical or other similar purposes may only be used on the radio 
frequencies and on the conditions determined in this Regulation. 
2 Objective of the Regulation 
The radio frequencies are used as this Regulation provides to safeguard the 
fair availability, efficient, appropriate and sufficiently interference-free use 
of radio frequencies. 
 
 
Regulation 
 
 
 
Reg. no TRAFICOM/265115/03.04.05.00/2025 

 2 (2) 
 
traficom.fi 
3 Definitions 
The Frequency Allocation Table, as given in annex, contains provisions on 
the allocation of radio frequencies, frequency bands and sub-bands for 
different purposes of use. The radio interface requirements and the 
frequency bands designated for ISM equipment, and the terms of use of 
this equipment, referred to in section 1, are also included in the Frequency 
Allocation Table. 
4 Entry into force 
This Regulation enters into force on 31 March 2026 and will remain in force 
until further notice. 
This Regulation repeals the Regulation bearing the same title (The Finnish 
Transport and Communications Agency 4 AF/2025M) issued by the Finnish 
Transport and Communications Agency on 27 March 2025. 
Helsinki on 26 March 2026

31.3.2026 Annex to Radio Frequency Regulation 
 1 (2) 
Amendments made to the Radio Frequency Regulation 4 AF/2025M since 31 March 2025 
 
 
 
Fixed service 
In the 8 GHz frequency band for radio links, the channel plan for 56 MHz channels has been 
extended from three to five channels. The use of mobile programme transmission links in the 
frequency band has ended.  
In the 15 GHz frequency band for radio links, the comment "No new licences" has been added for 
14 MHz channels with 308 MHz duplex separation. 
In the 23 GHz frequency band for radio links, the channel plan for 56 MHz channels has been 
extended from two to nine channels.  
In the 38 GHz frequency band for radio links, the channel plans for 3.5 MHz, 7 MHz and 14 MHz 
channels have been removed. 
Land mobile service 
The names of sub-band usage types have been changed. The purpose of the changes was to 
extend the range of uses allowed for the sub-bands, further specify current uses and simplify the 
names. The following changes have been made: 
 
"Control, alarm, telemetry, telecommand, data transmission" → "Data transfer for business 
activities" 
"PMR, hobby and professional activities (RHA68)" → "Radiotelephony (RHA68)" 
"PMR" → "Radiotelephony for business activities" 
"PMR (taxis)" → "Radiotelephony for business activities" 
"PMR (power utilities)" → "Radiotelephony for energy suppliers" 
"Digital PMR (DMR, dPMR)" → "Digital radiotelephony for business activities" 
 
The possibility of simplex use has been added for the sub-band 78.100–80.000 MHz. Such use was 
already allowed in the paired duplex frequencies 84.100 –86.00 MHz. 
 
The separate sub-bands 75.225–77.750 MHz / 80.225–82.750 MHz have been combined into one 
sub-band. 
 
The separate sub-bands 146.80625–148.26875 MHz / 151.40625–152.86875 MHz have been 
combined into one sub-band. 
 
In the frequency band 154–174 MHz, the primary service "Mobile" of the sub-bands 154.00625–
154.49375 MHz, 154.50625–154.64375 MHz and 154.65625–154.89375 MHz has been replaced 
with the more specific primary service "Land mobile". 
 
The separate sub-bands 448.78125–449.51875 MHz have been combined into one sub-band. 
Aeronautical mobile  
The use "national common frequency for aerodrome control and approach control" has been 
removed from the frequency 119.700 MHz. 
Satellite service 
A primary allocation to "Earth Exploration-Satellite" has been added in the sub-band 8025–8400 
MHz. 
 
Amateur radio 
The amateur radio sub-bands 433.000–433.375 MHz / 433.3875 MHz as well as the sub-bands 
434.600–434.975 MHz / 434.9875 MHz with 1.6 MHz duplex separation have been removed. 
For the frequencies of amateur radio 29.600 MHz, 51.510 MHz, 145.500 MHz and 433.500 MHz, 
comments have been added regarding FM calling frequencies. 
31.3.2026 Annex to Radio Frequency Regulation 
 2 (2) 
Amendments made to the Radio Frequency Regulation 4 AF/2025M since 31 March 2025 
 
 
 
References to ECC Decision ECC/DEC/(25)01 and ITU Recommendation ITU-R M.2164 on the 
protection of GNSS have been added in the comments for the frequency band 1240 –1300 MHz. 
Short-range devices 
Text updated in accordance with Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2025/650. The 
frequency band for wide-band data transmission equipment has been extended to include the sub-
band 916.4–919.4 MHz and the frequency band for non-specific short-range devices extended to 
include the sub-band 916.1–919.4 MHz.  
The text has been changed to include a reference to Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 
2025/913, which concerns wide-band data transmission equipment in the frequency band 5945 –
6425 MHz. 
Government 
The sub-band 738–753 MHz has been allocated to use by public authorities. The sub-band usage 
plan is still under review. 
Military use 
The allocation to "Mobile" corresponding to military use in the sub-band 230–240 MHz has been 
changed from secondary to primary use. 
Broadcasting 
The primary allocation to "Broadcasting" in the frequency band 230–267 MHz has been removed.

Inductive equipment, NMR equipment, ultra-wideband 
equipment (UWB) and wide-band data transmission equipment 
(WAS/RLAN) 57–71 GHz, security scanners and Amateur radio 
transmitters 
1 Inductive equipment 
The frequency bands in the 100 Hz – 30 MHz frequency range typically assigned for 
inductive equipment in Europe are listed in the ECC Recommendation ERC/REC 70-03 
on the use of Short Range Equipment (https://cept.org/eco/). Inductive equipment 
complying with Recommendation ERC/REC 70-03 and European Commission Decision 
2006/771/EC supplemented by Implementing Decision (EU) 2025/105 may be used in 
Finland. The use of other inductive equipment that meet the requirements of standard 
EN 300 330 or another similar European harmonised standard on inductive equipment 
and whose conformity has been verified in accordance with section 255 of the Act on 
Electronic Communications Services is not restricted in Finland, either. For licence -
exempt equipment, see Finnish Transport and Communications Agency Regulation 15. 
2 NMR equipment 
Enclosed Nuclear Magnetic Resonance (NMR) equipment in the frequency range 9 kHz 
– 130 MHz in accordance with Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2025/105. 
NMR equipment can be used to investigate the properties of different materials, for 
example. For licence-exempt equipment, see Finnish Transport and Communications 
Agency Regulation 15.

3.12 Amateur radio transmitters 
For justified reasons and for experimental purposes, the radio licence may entitle a 
radio amateur of general class to use higher transmitter power in a frequency band 
assigned for amateur radio communication than stipulated in the Frequency Allocation 
Table. The terms of an amateur radio station licence may also contain exceptions as 
to the provisions on amateur radio transmitters in this Table.

31.3.2026  Appendix 1 to Frequency Allocation Table 
 1 (3) 
DEFINITIONS 
 
 
 
 
Frequency band. Services in Finland 
 
Frequency band and services in use or intended to be used in this frequency band in 
Finland. The frequency bands and services are based on the Radio Regulations (RR) 
and the ERC Report 25. 
 
In the Frequency Allocation Table, primary services are written with upper case letters 
(e.g. MOBILE) and secondary services with lower case letters (e.g. Mobile). 
 
Sub-band, its width and usage 
 
Sub-bands, their width and intended use. In mobile and fixed services, the centre 
frequencies of the extreme channels are the lower and upper limits of a sub-band. In 
other radio services, the sub-band limits form the limits for the given usage. 
 
Mode of traffic 
 
Mode of traffic of a sub-band is either simplex (use of one frequency) or duplex (use 
of two frequencies). 
 
Class of station 
 
Class of station is based on the Radio Regulations (RR). In the land mobile service, for 
instance, the class of station of a base station is FB.  
 
Direction 
 
Defines the direction of transmission, i.e. whether the frequency is used for 
transmitting (TX) or receiving (RX) or both (TXRX).  
 
Channel width 
 
States the frequency separation between the centre frequencies of two adjacent 
channels. 
 
Bandwidth 
 
States the bandwidth allowed for a transmission using the channel (i.e. necessary 
bandwidth).  
 
Class of emission 
 
Determines, for instance, type of modulation and type of information to be 
transmitted. 
 
Duplex separation and paired band 
 
The corresponding frequency band (paired band) is situated at the distance given by 
the duplex separation either on higher frequencies (+) or on lower frequencies (-) 
than the band given in the table. 
 
Standard type 
 
Gives information on the most essential properties of radio link equipment (e.g. DRS 
34/18000 = capacity 34 Mbit/s, frequency range 18000 MHz or FM 4/419 = 
modulation FM, capacity 4speech channels and frequency range 419 MHz). 
 
31.3.2026  Appendix 1 to Frequency Allocation Table 
 2 (3) 
DEFINITIONS 
 
 
 
 
Radiated power 
 
The sum of the transmitter power and the antenna gain subtracted by the attenuation 
of the transmission lines is the radio transmitter's radiated power. The maximum 
radiated power is stated as W ERP units when it is compared to a dipole antenna (gain 
dBd) or as W EIRP units when it is compared to an isotropic antenna (gain dBi).  
 
Data transmission 
 
Data transmission means the transmission by radio of any communication other than 
real-time person-to-person communication. Real-time person-to-person 
communication means voice, video or other direct interaction in which the parties 
exchange information such that any delay in the interaction results solely from the 
transmission technology used to establish the connection. Data transmission may 
include, for example, the transmission of telemetry data, the control of a remote 
device with or without line-of-sight, or other similar activity. 
 
Duty cycle 
 
The duty cycle is defined as the ratio of the maximum transmitter "on" time, relative 
to a one-hour period. 
 
Output power of radio link 
 
If no maximum output power is mentioned for the transmitter of a radio link, the 
value given in the standard reference is applicable. The standard reference concerning 
the radiation pattern envelope of a radio link antenna defines the required maximum 
side lobe attenuation, which can be relaxed depending on the usage environment of 
the system in question. 
 
Radio Regulations, RR 
 
The mandatory (binding) Annex to the Constitution and Convention of the 
International Telecommunications Union (ITU Radio Regulations). 
 
Radio transmitters with a high risk of interference  
 
Radio transmitters that present a high risk of interference and require a radio licence 
to possess and operate include sound radio transmitters intended for analogue 
broadcasting, television and radio transmitters intended for digital television or radio 
broadcasting, and high-power radars.  
 
In addition to those referred to above, radio transmitters with a high risk of 
interference also include transmitters for which Traficom has granted a licence for 
their possession and operation for research and development purposes, even though it 
has not been confirmed that the transmitter meets the requirements laid down in 
sections 251 and 264 of the Act on Electronic Communications Services. 
 
The licence terms for a radio transmitter that present a high risk of interference may 
stipulate that the transmitter must not be put into service until Traficom has approved 
its commissioning on the basis of an inspection (Act on Electronic Communications 
Services, section 42). 
 
References to standards 
 
The standard references are only for informative purposes and they do not set 
compulsory requirements for placing of equipment on the market. When there are 
31.3.2026  Appendix 1 to Frequency Allocation Table 
 3 (3) 
DEFINITIONS 
 
 
 
references to standards or other comparable specifications in the Radio Frequency 
Plan, this implies that they have been used as assumptions for equipment 
performance in an interference analysis concerning a new frequency assignment or as 
a technical basis for compatibility studies between different radio communications 
services or as a technical basis for coordination agreements with other countries. 
Standard references may in some cases also be used to define a channel access 
procedure, the use of which is a condition for the use of certain frequency bands. 
 
The standard references do not specify the version of the standard. Reference means 
the latest version published in the Official Journal of the European Union.

STANDARDS FOR PMR EQUIPMENT  
 
 
 
The standard references are only for informative purposes and they do not set compulsory 
requirements for placing of equipment on the market. When there are references to 
standards or other comparable specifications in the Radio Frequency Plan, this implie s that 
they have been used as assumptions for equipment performance in an interference analysis 
concerning a new frequency assignment or as a technical basis for compatibility studies 
between different radio communications services or as a technical basis for coordination 
agreements with other countries. Standard references may in some cases also be used to 
define a channel access procedure, the use of which is a condition for the use of certain 
frequency bands. 
 
The standard references do not specify the version of the standard. Reference means the 
latest version published in the Official Journal of the European Union.

 3. Telecommand and Telemetry Equipment and Data Transmission Systems 
 
  3.1 Standard EN 300 220 or EN 302 561 with the following specifications:  
 
   These standards are applied to equipment with transmission power below 0.5 W in 
the frequency bands 29.810–29.940 MHz and 161.4125–161.4625 MHz and in the 
sub-bands within the frequency band 406–470 MHz identified by Finnish Transport 
and Communication Agency for this purpose. 
 
  3.2 Standard EN 300 113 or standard EN 302 561 applies to all other frequency 
ranges than those mentioned above, or to equipment with a transmission power 
exceeding 0.5 W.

These frequencies are used for ship-to-ship communications. When required, they can be used also for
